This can happen when a component repeatedly calls setState inside componentWillUpdate or componentDidUpdate. React limits the number of  \r\n  nested updates to prevent infinite loops.                                                                                                                                      \r\n      at setRef (composeRefs.tsx:11:5)                                                                                                                                           \r\n      at composeRefs.tsx:22:45                                                                                                                                                   \r\n      at Array.forEach                                                                                                                                       \r\n      at setRef (composeRefs.tsx:11:5)                                                                                               \r\n      ...                                                                                                                                                                        \r\n                                                                                                                                                                                 \r\n  The setRef function in @radix-ui/react-compose-refs recursively triggers itself, causing React to hit the maximum update depth limit.                                          \r\n                                                                                                                                                                                 \r\n  In React 19, ref callback behavior changed - ref callbacks can now return cleanup functions, and React may call ref callbacks differently than in React 18. When setRef calls  \r\n  ref(value) at line 11, it triggers a re-render which invokes setRef again via the composed ref callback at line 22, creating an infinite loop.    \r\n\r\n\r\n### Expected behavior\r\n\r\n  Composed refs should work without causing infinite update loops in React 19.                                                                                                    \r\n\r\n### Reproducible example\r\n\r\nI have platejs plugins with radix popovers and radix tooltips on them. If I write fast inside plate, the page breaks. \r\nI\'m on a Macbook M1 Pro with 32 GB. One way to trigger this error is to inspect chrome and start a performance recording and start typing real fast. On Safari, for example, it doesn\'t break. I tried on restarted browser, guest mode. I\'m not the only one\r\n\r\n[CodeSandbox Template](n/a)\r\n\r\n### Suggested solution\r\n\r\n  The composeRefs and useComposedRefs functions need to handle React 19\'s new ref callback behavior. Possible approaches:                                                        \r\n                                                                                                                                                                                 \r\n  1. Memoize the composed ref callback to prevent unnecessary re-invocations                                                                                                     \r\n  2. Add a guard to prevent recursive setRef calls                                                                                                                               \r\n  3. Handle the cleanup function return value from React 19 ref callbacks       \r\n\r\n### Additional context\r\nRight now, it works fine with react 18 (even on next.js 16+) and we had to downgrade.\r\n\r\nI also saw this reddit post https://www.reddit.com/r/react/comments/1nnvwsp/react_19_causes_maximum_update_depth_exceeded/ where a user raised a similar case\r\n\r\nReact 19 introduced changes to ref callback behavior, including support for cleanup functions returned from ref callbacks. This may be causing the ref to be re-invoked on each render cycle.                                                                                                                                                                 \r\n                                                                                                                                                                                 \r\n  Related stack trace also shows Slate editor involvement:                                                                                                                       \r\n  at Slate.useCallback[onContextChange] (slate.tsx:62:7)                                                                                                                         \r\n  at e.onChange                                                                                                                                              \r\n                                                                                                                                                                                 \r\n  This suggests the issue manifests when Radix components are used {'role': 'assistant', 'content': [{'type': 'thinking', 'thinking': 'Now I understand the history:\n\n1. There was a fix (PR before #3554) that changed `createSlot` (in its `SlotClone` form at the time) to use `useComposedRefs(childrenRef, forwardedRef)` — a memoized composed ref. This made "function refs should be called only once" pass.\n\n2. However, that fix caused an **RSC error**: adding `\'use client\'` to `packages/react/slot/src/index.ts` didn\'t fully resolve it. What was the RSC error? Using the `useComposedRefs` hook (which calls `React.useCallback`) inside the Slot render — in RSC, hooks aren\'t allowed... actually, is Radix\'s Slot used in Server Components? No wait — the issue is: in RSC, the `Slot` component is imported into a Server Component and rendered there (statically). `React.forwardRef` + hooks in an RSC component → error "useCallback is not a hook supported in Server Components", or the component simply can\'t use hooks.\n\nWait, but Slot is a client component... The problem in RSC: even when using a Radix component in an RSC app, the *server* render path executes the component function for serialization purposes? No — RSC doesn\'t execute client component render functions on the server; it just references them. But wait, Radix\'s `Primitive` etc. are client components, so that shouldn\'t be an issue.\n\nHmm, the actual RSC error: adding `\'use client\'` to index.ts changed how the module boundary works. The revert removed both the `\'use client\'` directive and the `useComposedRefs` usage.\n\nThe RSC error with `useComposedRefs` inside Slot: if the Slot component (a client component) is rendered inside a server component tree... it still becomes a client render. Hmm, but there\'s a subtle point: **`React.forwardRef` components with hooks are fine in RSC as long as the module is marked \'use client\'**. The original fix (pre-revert) didn\'t have \'use client\' on the slot index.ts? No wait — looking at the revert diff: it removes `\'use client\';` from index.ts and reverts slot.tsx to a non-hook version. So the original fix PR was: (a) add \'use client\' to index.ts, (b) use useComposedRefs. And it caused RSC errors.\n\nWhat were those RSC errors? Probably: adding \'use client\' to the slot package made the Slot/Slottable exports client-only — if a user imports `Slottable` in a server component file (or if it\'s transitively imported there), it errors. Actually, more specifically, I recall an issue where \'use client\' on `@radix-ui/react-slot` broke because `createSlot` is used in `@radix-ui/react-primitive` — if primitive imports from a \'use client\' module, the whole primitive becomes a client component. WeakMap<array, fn> works (arrays are objects) but the array key is garbage-collectable after the first render (no strong ref) — that\'s fine: the entry vanishes from the WeakMap and we just recreate the closure on the next render. WeakMap semantics: a weak reference is alive only if the key is reachable elsewhere. The array is only referenced from the WeakMap → GC\'d → the cache entry disappears → we recreate the closure on the next render → identity changes → exactly the bug we\'re trying to fix!\n\nSo a WeakMap keyed by the pair is no good. Same GC problem at the second level.\n\nWhat if we key only by slottableElementRef, but the cached closure needs to call the "current" forwardedRef? When using `asChild` in an RSC environment, especially with React 19, issues arise because:\n1.  **Lazy References:** In production builds, RSC serialization may turn components into "lazy" references (e.g., `Symbol(react.lazy)`). Older versions of `Slot` do not handle these correctly, causing the component to fail to render.\n2.  **Version Mismatches:** Even if you have `@radix-ui/react-slot@1.2.4` installed, other Radix packages (like `@radix-ui/react-dialog`) may still resolve to `1.2.3` as a transitive dependency, leaving your application using the older, buggy version.\n3.  **Silent Failures:** In some environments (specifically post-Next.js 16 security patches), `Slot` can fail to merge props silently, resulting in components simply disappearing from the DOM without an error message.\n\n### Recommended Fixes\n\n#### 1. Force the Correct Version (Primary Solution)\nTo ensure all Radix primitives use the version of `Slot` that contains the necessary fixes, you should force the resolution of `@radix-ui/react-slot` to `1.2.4` (or the latest stable) in your `package.json`.\n\n**For pnpm:**\n```json\n{\n  "pnpm": {\n    "overrides": {\n      "@radix-ui/react-slot": "1.2.4"\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n**For npm:**\n```json\n{\n  "overrides": {\n    "@radix-ui/react-slot": "1.2.4"\n  }\n}\n```\n\n**For yarn:**\n```json\n{\n  "resolutions": {\n    "@radix-ui/react-slot": "1.2.4"\n  }\n}\n```\n\n#### 2. Verify "use client" Usage\nEnsure that any component using `Slot` (or any component that composes `Slot`, like a custom `Button`) is marked with `\'use client\'` at the very top of the file.\n*   **Why:** `Slot` relies on React features (like `ref` forwarding and complex prop merging) that are intended for the client-side runtime. Even if the *parent* page is a Server Component, the component invoking `Slot` must be a Client Component.\n\n#### 3. {'response': {'provider': 'gemini', 'answer': 'The issues you are encountering with `@radix-ui/react-slot` (specifically around version 1.2.4) in React Server Components (RSC) are primarily due to **serialization gaps** and **dependency resolution mismatches** between Radix UI packages.\n\n### The Core Problem\nThe `Slot` component is designed to merge props onto its child. When using `asChild` in an RSC environment, especially with React 19, issues arise because:\n1.  **Lazy References:** In production builds, RSC serialization may turn components into "lazy" references (e.g., `Symbol(react.lazy)`). Older versions of `Slot` do not handle these correctly, causing the component to fail to render.\n2.  **Version Mismatches:** Even if you have `@radix-ui/react-slot@1.2.4` installed, other Radix packages (like `@radix-ui/react-dialog`) may still resolve to `1.2.3` as a transitive dependency, leaving your application using the older, buggy version.\n3.  **Silent Failures:** In some environments (specifically post-Next.js 16 security patches), `Slot` can fail to merge props silently, resulting in components simply disappearing from the DOM without an error message.\n\n### Recommended Fixes\n\n#### 1. Force the Correct Version (Primary Solution)\nTo ensure all Radix primitives use the version of `Slot` that contains the necessary fixes, you should force the resolution of `@radix-ui/react-slot` to `1.2.4` (or the latest stable) in your `package.json`.\n\n**For pnpm:**\n```json\n{\n  "pnpm": {\n    "overrides": {\n      "@radix-ui/react-slot": "1.2.4"\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n**For npm:**\n```json\n{\n  "overrides": {\n    "@radix-ui/react-slot": "1.2.4"\n  }\n}\n```\n\n**For yarn:**\n```json\n{\n  "resolutions": {\n    "@radix-ui/react-slot": "1.2.4"\n  }\n}\n```\n\n#### 2. Human verification of the fix and its implications is still outstanding.\n\n## Summary\n\n- `SlotClone` now uses `useComposedRefs()` (memoized via `React.useCallback`) instead of raw `composeRefs()` to compose forwarded and children refs\n- This keeps the composed ref callback identity stable across renders, preventing React 19\'s ref unmount/remount cycle from triggering infinite loops\n\n## Reproduction\n\n**Minimal repro repo**: https://github.com/yannisgu/radix-tanstack-form-react19-repro\n\n```bash\ngit clone https://github.com/yannisgu/radix-tanstack-form-react19-repro\ncd radix-tanstack-form-react19-repro/radix-select-tanstack-form\npnpm install && pnpm dev\n# Open http://localhost:5173 — tab freezes\n```\n\n## Problem\n\n`SlotClone` calls `composeRefs(forwardedRef, childrenRef)` directly on every render, creating a **new function** each time. In React 19, `setRef()` returns `ref(value)`, which React treats as a cleanup function. When React sees a new callback ref with cleanup on each render, it unmounts the old ref (calling cleanup) and mounts the new one — triggering any state setters passed as refs (e.g. `SelectTrigger`\'s `onTriggerChange`, or `Checkbox`\'s `useSize`).\n\nThis state update during commit causes a re-render, which creates another new `composeRefs` function, which triggers another unmount/remount — an infinite synchronous loop that **freezes the browser tab**.\n\nThe bug is particularly severe when combined with libraries that trigger re-renders during the commit phase (e.g. `@tanstack/react-form`\'s `useField` layout effect with no dependency array), but can occur with any component that has effects or state updates during commit.\n\n## Root cause chain\n\n1. `SlotClone` calls `composeRefs()` → new function identity every render\n2. `setRef(ref, value)` returns `ref(value)` → React 19 treats it as cleanup ref\n3. New ref identity + cleanup → React unmounts old ref, mounts new ref\n4. Ref mount triggers state setter (e.g. `onTriggerChange`) → re-render\n5. Re-render → step 1 → infinite loop\n\n## Fix\n\nReplace raw `composeRefs()` with `useComposedRefs()`, which wraps the call in `React.useCallback`. This was already the intended pattern — `useComposedRefs` exists for exactly this purpose but wasn\'t used in `SlotClone`.\n\n## Affected components\n\nAny Radix component using `Slot`/`asChild` that passes a state setter as a ref:\n- `@radix-ui/react-select` (SelectTrigger + onTriggerChange)\n- `@radix-ui/react-checkbox` (useSize state setter)\n- `@radix-ui/react-popover`, `@radix-ui/react-tooltip`, `@radix-ui/react-dialog`, etc.\n\nFixes #3799\n\n\nWall time: 0.85 seconds'}], 'details': {'timeoutSeconds': 300, 'wallTimeMs': 853.4422999999952}, 'isError': False, 'timestamp': 1787265533178}

{'role': 'assistant', 'content': [{'type': 'thinking', 'thinking': 'Now I have the full picture:\n\n**Root cause** (from PR #3804, fixing issue #3799):\n1. `Slot`/`SlotClone` calls `composeRefs(forwardedRef, childrenRef)` on every render → a new function identity is produced on every render.\n2. In React 19, `setRef` returns `ref(value)` — if the user\'s ref callback returns a cleanup function (React 19 feature), the composed ref becomes a "callback ref with cleanup."\n3. New ref identity per render → React 19 detaches the old ref (calls cleanup) and attaches the new one on every render.\n4. Ref attachment invokes state setters passed as refs (e.g., `SelectTrigger`\'s `onTriggerChange`, `useSize`, or Slate\'s editor ref) → setState during commit → re-render → new composeRefs → … infinite loop → "Maximum update depth exceeded" / browser freeze.\n\n**Fix** (PR #3804, already merged into current upstream main): use `useComposedRefs(forwardedRef, slottableElementRef)` — memoized via `React.useCallback` — so the composed callback identity stays stable while the refs are stable. No \'use client\' directive (that was the RSC breakage in #3554).\n\nNote the details of the fix in current upstream main (post-#3729 structure, which matches our snapshot\'s structure):\n\n```tsx\nconst slottableElementRef = slottableElement ? getElementRef(slottableElement) : undefined;\nconst composedRef = useComposedRefs(forwardedRef, slottableElementRef);\n\nif (!slottableElement) {\n  if (children || children === 0) {\n    throw new Error(\n      hasSlottable ? createSlottableError(ownerName) : createSlotError(ownerName),\n    );\n  }\n  return children;\n}\n\nconst mergedProps = mergeProps(slotProps, slottableElement.props ?? {});\n\n// do not pass ref to React.Fragment for React 19 compatibility\nif (slottableElement.type !== React.Fragment) {\n  mergedProps.ref = forwardedRef ? composedRef : slottableElementRef;\n}\n\nreturn React.cloneElement(slottableElement, mergedProps);\n```\n\nKey points: `useComposedRefs` must be called unconditionally (rules of hooks — before the early `return children` / `throw`). In our snapshot, `slottableElementRef` is computed after the null check, so we need to hoist it up with a ternary `slottableElement ? getElementRef(slottableElement) : undefined` and compute it before the hook call.\n\nWait — one subtlety: in our snapshot, when `slottableElement` is null and children is empty, it does `return children` (an early return before the hook call). The hook must be called before that return. So:\n\n```tsx\nconst slottableElementRef = slottableElement ? getElementRef(slottableElement) : undefined;\nconst composedRefs = useComposedRefs(forwardedRef, slottableElementRef);\n\nif (!slottableElement) {\n  ...throw or return children;\n}\n```\n\nAlso, note `mergedProps.ref = forwardedRef ? composedRefs : slottableElementRef;` — hmm, when there\'s no forwardedRef, we still use slottableElementRef directly (its identity is stable as long as the child element keeps the same ref). No problem.\n\nBut wait — there\'s an edge case: when `forwardedRef` exists and the child has no ref: `composedRefs = useComposedRefs(forwardedRef, undefined)` — memoized, stable. React attaches only once.\n\nEdge case: `useComposedRefs`\'s implementation:\n\n```tsx\nfunction useComposedRefs<T>(...refs: PossibleRef<T>[]): React.RefCallback<T> {\n  return React.useCallback(composeRefs(...refs), refs);\n}\n```\n\n`React.useCallback(fn, refs)` — `refs` here is the spread rest array `[forwardedRef, slottableElementRef]`. A new array object is produced on every render, but useCallback compares deps element-by-element with Object.is: forwardedRef and slottableElementRef. If both are referentially stable across renders → same callback is returned.\n\nOne caveat: `composeRefs(...refs)` is called on every render (to produce `fn`), but useCallback discards it if the deps haven\'t changed. Slight wasted work, but that\'s the upstream implementation — no problem.\n\nNow — what about the RSC concern? The #3804 fix (merged) does not add \'use client\'. The current upstream main has no \'use client\' in slot\'s index. So this is the accepted fix.\n\nBut wait, I need to double-check the `useComposedRefs` signature/exports in this snapshot\'s compose-refs package — yes, it\'s exported from compose-refs.tsx (`export { composeRefs, useComposedRefs };`), and I need to verify that the slot package\'s package.json depends on @radix-ui/react-compose-refs.\n\nAlso: is the `useComposedRefs` import already present in slot.tsx? No — the snapshot imports `{ composeRefs }`. I need to change the import to `{ useComposedRefs }`. And `composeRefs` may no longer be used in slot.tsx — check: line 75 `composeRefs(forwardedRef, slottableElementRef)` is the only usage. So the import becomes `{ useComposedRefs }`.\n\nNow, before the fix — reproducing. Steps:\n\n1. Unskip the `describe.skip(\'given an Input\')` test and confirm it fails (that\'s the existing regression test for this bug).\n2. Additionally, write a "Maximum update depth exceeded" reproduction test matching the reported bug: a ref callback that returns a cleanup function (React 19) + a state setter, wrapped by a re-rendering Slot → expect "Maximum update depth exceeded" to be thrown before the fix and pass after.\n\nWait, actually, let me think about what the minimal "Maximum update depth exceeded" repro looks like:\n\nPer PR #3804\'s explanation: the loop requires the composed ref to be a "callback ref with cleanup" — i.e., one of the composed refs returns a function (React 19 cleanup) — no, re-reading: "In React 19, setRef() returns ref(value), which React treats as a cleanup function."\n\nHmm, that\'s a misstatement: React 19 treats the return value of a ref callback as a cleanup only if it\'s a function. `setRef` returns `ref(value)` — the user\'s ref callback\'s return value. If the user\'s ref callback returns nothing (undefined), there\'s no cleanup. So the cleanup path is relevant when the user\'s ref returns a function.\n\nBut the loop can happen even without cleanup: a new ref identity every render → old ref called with null, new ref called with node. If the ref is a state setter `setOpen`, then `setOpen(null)` followed by `setOpen(node)` — null≠node → re-render… but then it converges (on the next render: null → node, converges again? no wait).\n\nLet me trace: render 1 (initial): ref attached: setOpen(null)→setOpen(node). State change → render 2: new composeRef → React: old ref (null): setOpen(null); new ref: setOpen(node) → render 3: same as render 2 → setOpen(null), setOpen(node) → render 4… each render produces two setState calls with changing values (null ↔ node) → each pair produces a new render → infinite loop! Right! Because the ref identity changes every render, React detaches (null) and reattaches (node) on every commit, and setState(null) followed by setState(node) — each pair is a state change from the previous commit\'s final value (node) → null → node. React batches: within one commit\'s ref phase, setOpen(null) then setOpen(node) → final state = node, same as previous → React might bail out!\n\nHmm, actually, React 18+ batches these two updates (same event/commit phase) → computed final value = node → Object.is(node, node) → bailout → no re-render → no loop. That\'s why the simple case doesn\'t loop.\n\nFor a true loop you need the state to change on every iteration. Per PR #3804\'s repro: tanstack-form\'s useField layout effect with no dependency array → runs after every commit → setState during commit… or SelectTrigger\'s onTriggerChange:\n\nActually, let me look at the real repro repo: https://github.com/yannisgu/radix-tanstack-form-react19-repro — SelectTrigger + tanstack form. Mechanism per the PR body: "triggers a state setter passed as a ref (e.g., SelectTrigger\'s onTriggerChange, or Checkbox\'s useSize). A state update during commit causes re-render, which produces yet another new composeRefs function, which triggers another unmount/remount — an infinite synchronous loop."\n\nSo the loop: ref callback calls setState (e.g., `onTriggerChange(node)` — stores the trigger element, or a size measurement `setSize(...)` that produces a new object each time, or a `useSize` hook that sets `{width, height}`). A new object per measurement → state always changes → re-render → new composeRefs → ref detached/reattached → measures again → new object → loop.\n\nSimplest in-test repro:\n\n```tsx\nfunction Child() {\n  const [size, setSize] = React.useState({ w: 0, h: 0 });\n  const ref = (node: HTMLInputElement | null) => {\n    if (node) setSize({ w: node.offsetWidth, h: node.offsetHeight }); // new object each time\n    return () => {}; // React 19 cleanup\n  };\n  ...\n}\n```\n\nHmm wait, but setSize with a new object {0,0} — a new object per call → always a change → re-render. And the re-render produces a new composeRefs closure (pre-fix) → ref reattached → setSize called again (in the ref attach phase — which happens during commit, before effects)…\n\nActually, where does the ref attach happen? During commit, in the layout phase (before useLayoutEffect). setState during that phase → synchronous re-render before browser paint (like useLayoutEffect). Loop: render → commit → ref attach → setState(new obj) → re-render → commit → ref detach+attach (new identity) → setState → … → "Maximum update depth exceeded".\n\nBut note: even with a stable ref identity (post-fix), does this loop? Post-fix: ref identity is stable → React doesn\'t re-call the ref on re-render → setSize is called only once → converges.\n\nAlso, is the returned cleanup function necessary? Without cleanup: React 19\'s rule for re-invoke is "ref callback identity changed." So a new closure per render is enough to retrigger, regardless of cleanup. The cleanup detail in PR #3804\'s explanation is somewhat muddled, but the core is: new identity per render → ref re-invoked per render → state setter in ref → loop.\n\nHmm wait, actually — hold on. Even in React 18, if the identity changes, the ref callback is called again with null and node on every re-render. So why doesn\'t the loop happen in React 18? The reporter says "works fine on react 18."\n\nThe difference: in React 18, `setRef` calls `ref(value)` and returns the result — in 1.1.0\'s composeRefs the composed callback returned undefined. Look at our snapshot\'s `setRef`:\n\n```tsx\nfunction setRef<T>(ref: PossibleRef<T>, value: T) {\n  if (typeof ref === \'function\') {\n    return ref(value);   // returns the user ref\'s return value\n  }\n  ...\n}\n```\n\nAnd composeRefs:\n\n```tsx\nfunction composeRefs<T>(...refs: PossibleRef<T>[]): React.RefCallback<T> {\n  return (node) => {\n    let hasCleanup = false;\n    const cleanups = refs.map((ref) => {\n      const cleanup = setRef(ref, node);\n      if (!hasCleanup && typeof cleanup == \'function\') {\n        hasCleanup = true;\n      }\n      return cleanup;\n    });\n    if (hasCleanup) {\n      return () => { ... };\n    }\n  };\n}\n```\n\nIf none of the user\'s refs returns a function, the composed ref returns undefined → React 19 sees a plain callback ref → re-invoke only when identity changes. Marking the todo as complete and summarizing:\n\n**Root cause**: `createSlot` (packages/react/slot/src/slot.tsx) was calling `composeRefs(forwardedRef, slottableElementRef)` directly during render, producing a new ref callback identity on every render. React re-invokes callback refs whenever the identity changes (detach: null/cleanup, re-attach: node). When one of the composed refs updates state on every invocation (Slate/Plate\'s editor ref, `useSize`, `SelectTrigger`\'s `onTriggerChange`), each re-attach sets state during the commit phase → re-render → new closure → re-attach → infinite loop → "Maximum update depth exceeded" (the reported error, stack goes through `setRef` in compose-refs). This is why it\'s React 19-specific and timing-dependent (fast typing in plate + Chrome), and why it was fine on React 18 in the reporter\'s setup.\n\n**Fix**: Use the memoized `useComposedRefs(forwardedRef, slottableElementRef)` (internally `React.useCallback`) so the composed callback\'s identity is stable while the inputs are stable → React no longer re-invokes the ref on every commit. The hook call is unconditional (hoisted above the early return / throw) to satisfy the Rules of Hooks. Intentionally not adding a `\'use client\'` directive — the previous attempt (#3554\'s revert) broke RSC by adding it; this matches the fix that upstream re-landed (#3804).\n\n**Files changed**:\n1. `packages/react/slot/src/slot.tsx` — the fix itself.\n2. `packages/react/slot/src/slot.test.tsx` — unskipped the existing `given an Input` ref test (TODO "unskip when root cause is fixed"); added a regression test that reproduces the reported crash ("Maximum update depth exceeded" via a stateful ref + a parent that re-renders on typing).\n3. `.changeset/stable-refs-slot.md` — changeset following the repo convention.\n\n**Verification**:\n- Before the fix: the unskipped test fails (ref called 7 times instead of 1); the new test throws `Error: Maximum update depth exceeded ... at setRef (compose-refs.tsx:11)` — an exact match with the reported error.\n- After the fix: `pnpm vitest run packages/react/slot` → 32/32 passing; full `pnpm vitest run` → 20 files, 258 passing, 1 todo; `tsc --noEmit` in slot; oxlint clean; oxfmt applied.\n', 'thinkingSignature': 'reasoning'}, {'type': 'text', 'text': '\n\nAll green.
